### Subsystem Registry Design Notes

The 10-subsystem layout reflects two first-class groups:

- **SS-01 through SS-04** are **Subsystem A** (Rust compiled artifacts). They share one Cargo workspace, one release binary per platform, and the WASM plugin ABI contract.
- **SS-05 through SS-10** are **Subsystem B** (VSDD orchestration framework). They share one plugin manifest, one marketplace entry, and the Lobster workflow format.

**Deviations from the suggested 10-subsystem template:**

- SS-05 was renamed from "Pipeline Orchestration" to "Pipeline Orchestration" — kept, but agents were moved in (34 agents belong here alongside workflows because agents are the steps' executors; splitting them would orphan the orchestrator).
- SS-06 "Skill Catalog" is the largest single BC surface (585 BCs as of v1.0.0-beta.6; see Subsystem Registry table for current count). It is intentionally a standalone subsystem because skills have independent behavioral contracts per skill (each SKILL.md is a discrete unit of behavior).
- SS-09 "Configuration and Activation" is narrower than the suggested name — it covers only the activation plumbing and CI variant generation, not general config. `hooks-registry.toml` routing lives in SS-07 (Hook Bash Layer) because it is the routing table for that layer.
- SS-10 merges "CLI Tools" and "Bin" because all 12 bin tools are CLI-invocable and the commands/ slash-command bindings are just thin wrappers around skills.

## Component Dependency Map

```mermaid
graph TD
    CC["Claude Code (harness)"]
    SS09["SS-09: Configuration and Activation"]
    SS01["SS-01: Hook Dispatcher Core"]
    SS02["SS-02: Hook SDK and Plugin ABI"]
    SS03["SS-03: Event Emission (OTel-Aligned)"]
    SS04["SS-04: Plugin Ecosystem"]
    SS05["SS-05: Pipeline Orchestration"]
    SS06["SS-06: Skill Catalog"]
    SS07["SS-07: Hook Bash Layer"]
    SS08["SS-08: Templates and Rules"]
    SS10["SS-10: CLI Tools and Bin"]

    CC -->|"reads hooks.json"| SS09
    SS09 -->|"generated variants wire to"| SS01
    SS01 -->|"loads + executes WASM via"| SS02
    SS01 -->|"writes all events to (single-stream)"| SS03
    SS01 -->|"invokes plugins from"| SS04
    SS04 -->|"shells out via exec_subprocess to"| SS07
    SS05 -->|"dispatches sub-agents who call"| SS06
    SS05 -->|"gate writes via"| SS07
    SS06 -->|"renders output from"| SS08
    SS06 -->|"emits events via"| SS10
    SS10 -->|"reads/writes state in"| SS05
```

**Strict dependency direction:** data flows down (CC → SS09 → SS01 → SS02/SS03/SS04 → SS07). The orchestration stack (SS-05/06/07/08/10) is a separate plane. SS-07 sits at the intersection: bash hooks are invoked by both SS-04 (via legacy-bash-adapter) and SS-05/SS-06 (as gates on tool use).

## Cross-Cutting Concerns

| Concern | Owner | Mechanism |
|---------|-------|-----------|
| Observability (single-stream) | SS-03 | `events-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` — all lifecycle + domain events (ADR-015). Debug file `dispatcher-internal-*.jsonl` opt-in via `VSDD_DEBUG_LOG=1`; ADR-007 amended. |
| Capability enforcement | SS-01 | Deny-by-default; cap-gated host fns emit denial event + return code |
| Schema versioning | SS-01, SS-09, SS-03 | per-config: `hooks-registry.toml` schema_version=2 (post-ADR-019; v1→v2 hard-error, no compat shim); `observability-config.toml` schema_version=2 (post-ADR-015 D-15.1; v1→v2 hard-errors with migration hint per BC-3.05.004 PC4); other TOML configs schema_version=1; mismatch = hard error per DI-014 |
| Trace correlation | SS-01 | `trace_id` (UUID v4) propagated on every emitted event (renamed from `dispatcher_trace_id` per DI-017 / ADR-015 v1.7) |
| Platform selection | SS-09 | Activation skill copies `hooks.json.<platform>` (ADR-009) |
| Error non-blocking | SS-01 | Registry/payload/engine errors → `internal.dispatcher_error` → exit 0 |
| Bash hook compatibility | SS-04 | `legacy-bash-adapter.wasm` via `exec_subprocess` (ADR-012) |
| Secrets protection | SS-07 | `protect-secrets.sh` PreToolUse gate + env_allow deny-by-default |

| ADR-030 | pr-manager merge-operation integrity enforcement — three-component mechanical enforcement for D-749 (stale-HEAD READY verdict race) and D-750 (release-PR squash-merge not mechanically enforced): (1) `pr-manager-completion-guard.wasm` SubagentStop advisory gate — READY verdict lacking `covered_sha` emits `READY_SHA_MISSING` advisory (`on_error="continue"` advisory-block-mode via stdout outcome; priority=920); (2) `bin/check-stale-verdict.sh` — compares `covered_sha` against live `gh pr view --json headRefOid`; exits STALE_READY_VERDICT on drift; orchestrator MUST invoke before any READY-verdict merge; fail-closed on gh failure; (3) `bin/enforce-merge-strategy.sh` — wraps `gh pr merge`; forces `--merge` for `^release/v` branches; exits RELEASE_PR_SQUASH_FORBIDDEN on `--squash`/`--rebase` attempt; (4) `release-branch-guardrail.yml` adjudicated NOT extendable (`pull_request` events carry no `merge_method` field); **Alternatives rejected:** Option B blocking SubagentStop stale-check rejected (broad `binary_allow` + fail-closed on gh auth destroys pr-manager output); Option C bash hook command-content match rejected (fragile); Option D prompt-only rejected per F-P2-002 (LLM prompt-spec unenforceable by bats); normative twin BC-5.42.001; CAP-033; rationale roots D-749+D-750+F-P2-002 — **ACCEPTED 2026-07-06; ADR-030 v1.0; E-19 adv-P3 F-P3-015 close-out; SS-04 (WASM plugin crate) + SS-05 (pr-manager agent domain) + SS-07 (hooks-registry SubagentStop entry) + SS-10 (bin tools); v1.1 2026-07-08 D-775: F-W1V-001..004 adjudications — bin/ path confirmed (orchestrator-invoked SS-10 CLI, precedent factory-lock-write.sh/factory-cas-push.sh); positional signatures adopted (spec-wins; T-003/T-004 assert); READY_SHA_FETCH_FAILED + READY_SHA_MISSING named codes adopted; CHECK_STALE_VERDICT_ERROR retained for EC-003 + unclassified arms; exit 1 adopted (S-19.01 AC-002/RG-003); v1.2 2026-07-08 D-776: F-P22-003 Decision 1 TOML tool filter ^Agent → ^Agent$ (fully-anchored singleton form per S-19.04 D-f convention; prevents substring match on AgentX-style tool names); v1.3 2026-07-08 D-777: F-P23-001 canonical TOML descriptor rewritten to live-registry shape — on_error="continue" advisory-block-mode via stdout outcome; priority=920; tool=/tier= fields REMOVED (not present in live SubagentStop entries); F-P22-003 superseded (^Agent$ was anchoring a field that doesn't exist); v1.4 2026-07-11 D-829: §D3 governed-pass-through wrapper contract (arch-wrapper 447bff71; S-21.01) + skill-doc merge pre-check BC-5.43.001 (Layer 2: git diff --name-only HEAD..<target> halts on .factory/ path); (2) crate naming MANDATORY: validate-factory-path-staging NOT validate-artifact-path (existing validate-artifact-path.wasm is Edit/Write/MultiEdit guard for BC-4.11.001 — functionally disjoint; Decision 3); (3) INV-E21-002+INV-E21-004 skill-doc (BC-6.26.001: absolute write-path anchoring + worktree teardown preflight; SS-06; S-21.04); (4) INV-E21-003 skill-doc (BC-6.27.001: 5-step factory-side PR restore + dispatch-preamble ASSERT branch==factory-artifacts; not documented in any single agent protocol file; server-side origination is the primary Layer-2 threat vector: contributor PR adding .factory/-pathed files merged server-side bypasses Layer-1 entirely, next git pull delivers tracked content); enforcement site = per-story-delivery.md §Main-Checkout Sync Protocol constraint (S-21.01 Layer-2 deliverable alongside WASM crate); Layer-1 scope narrow (git add/stage only; no extension to pull/merge); | ADR-032 | verify-state-timestamp-refresh — Edit/MultiEdit payload-targeted timestamp enforcement: Decision 1 — for Edit/MultiEdit, scan new_string values for top-level `timestamp:` OR `factory_lock:` fields; if neither is set in any new_string (payload-neutral — neither field), return `Continue` after skipping the timestamp and lock-expiry checks (Steps 4–7; AC-020 sentinel emitted); Decision 2 — Write tool path unchanged (full Steps 4–8 apply unconditionally); Decision 3 — lock-expiry check (Step 7) fires when `sets_timestamp==true` (always, regardless of `sets_factory_lock` — enforces PC4: timestamp-advancing Edit must also renew `factory_lock.expires_at` in same payload; Block(LockExpiryStale) if stale) OR when `sets_factory_lock==true` without `sets_timestamp` (Step 7 only; skip Steps 4–6); payload-neutral Edits skip Steps 4–7; Step 7 wrapped in `if sets_factory_lock \|\| sets_timestamp`; Decision 4 — new `pub fn new_string_sets_field(new_string: &str, field_key: &str) -> bool` helper (NEW function; 0 matches in crates/ pre-implementation); Decision 5 — AC-020 added to guard_logic function docstring `# BC traces` list. Second deliverable: AC-021 PostToolUse advisory sentinel per Bash git-commit events on factory-artifacts worktree; HEAD vs HEAD^ STATE.md `timestamp:` comparison via dispatcher-injected payload.extra['git_context'] fields (exec-free WASM; ADR-032-AC021-prereq; `on_error=continue`; no sidecar file). 11 tests (4 Red Gates + 7 regression guards). D-866 root-cause; Implementer Work Spec included. | ADR-037 | input-hash stable-input constraint — volatile cycle artifacts and catalog indexes must not be story hash inputs: Decision 1 — `inputs:` is a drift-detection mechanism, not a provenance log; a file belongs in `inputs:` only if a content change after story-authoring would require spec re-examination; Decision 2 — volatile artifact patterns MUST NOT appear in story `inputs:` arrays: `.factory/cycles/**/{decision-log,lessons,burst-log}.md`, `.factory/STATE.md`, `.factory/cycles/**/STATE.md`, `STORY-INDEX.md`, `BC-INDEX.md`, `ARCH-INDEX.md`; Decision 3 — provenance of specific D-NNN/L-NNN entries that informed a story belongs in `closes:`, `last_amended:`, and story body — not in `inputs:`; Decision 4 — BC-5.39.010 Class B MUST add volatile-input precondition (routed to product-owner): if `inputs:` contains §Decision 2 patterns, emit ADVISORY "Story [ID] has volatile inputs per ADR-037 §Decision 2 — three-way equality unsatisfiable until inputs corrected; Class B BLOCK suspended" and skip three-way equality check; Decision 5 — remediation sequence for 77 affected stories (originally 19 v1.0; expanded to 78 v1.1 by full-corpus re-derivation; S-21.07 remediated in pass-5 burst reducing to 77 v1.2): story-writer removes volatile entries; state-manager recomputes per-story hashes via `plugins/vsdd-factory/bin/compute-input-hash` `--update` single-file invocations (D-936 forbids `--scan --update`); S-19.01 stored hash `0ad9c4b` MUST NOT be corrected before story-writer removes `lessons.md` from its `inputs:` — correcting under pre-remediation semantics stores a hash that breaks again at the next lesson append; Decision 6 — `input-hash` field within a story file is NOT excluded from that file's content hash when the story is listed as an input to another story; full-file content hashing is correct for story-to-story spec dependencies; Decision 7 — frozen `adv-cycle-pass-N.md` and wave plan files are write-once by construction; 23 stories referencing only frozen artifacts are stable and require no remediation (corpus-verified 2026-08-05). | ADR-039 | Validator failure policy for resource exhaustion — per-plugin `failure_policy` field, fail-closed default for authorization-class validators, safe migration ordering: Decision 1 — `on_error` (crash behavior) and `failure_policy` (resource-exhaustion behavior) are separate non-unified axes; `failure_policy = "fail-closed" \| "fail-open"` as new per-plugin TOML field orthogonal to `on_error`; absent = fail-open (backward compatible during migration window); Decision 2 — per-plugin scope (not global); validator-class write-gating plugins MUST receive `failure_policy = "fail-closed"` after calibration, bifurcated by adapter class per v1.8 amendment (§AMD-001): native-WASM (validate-cross-site-correspondence, fuel-axis calibration sufficient) vs legacy-bash-adapter.wasm-hosted (validate-factory-path-root, validate-input-hash, validate-template-compliance, validate-wave-gate-prerequisite, validate-pr-merge-prerequisites — fuel-axis calibration necessary but NOT sufficient; host-wall-clock-timeout-axis (timeout_ms) calibration additionally required, since their bash subprocess is invisible to the WASM fuel counter per ADR-042 §Decision 3 class (b)); advisory-class plugins remain fail-open; Decision 3 — hard migration ordering constraint: four phases: (P1) schema extension, enforcement unchanged; (P2) near-term mitigations; (P3) per-plugin calibration bifurcated by adapter class — native-WASM: fuel-cap at p99×1.5 against production-scale corpus (lessons.md ≥4000 lines (D-442(e) hard limit; soft limit is 3500), STATE.md, decision-log.md, ≥574 KB fixture); legacy-bash-adapter.wasm-hosted: additionally timeout_ms at p99_ms×2.0 against the same corpus (v1.8 amendment); explicit self-lock statement for the two PreToolUse ^Agent$ gates (validate-wave-gate-prerequisite, validate-pr-merge-prerequisites) — fail-closed MUST NOT be applied on fuel_cap sufficiency alone; (P4) fail-closed annotation atomic with calibrated fuel_cap (and, for legacy-bash-adapter.wasm-hosted plugins, timeout_ms) — no half-state on either axis; lessons.md validators blocked until D-442(e) budget calibrated; Decision 4 — fuel budgeting: Option A static per-plugin cap max(p99×1.5, 50M) required for migration (native-WASM plugins); parallel host-wall-clock-timeout-axis formula timeout_ms ≥ max(p99_ms×2.0, 30_000) for legacy-bash-adapter.wasm-hosted plugins (v1.8 amendment; v1.9: reframed as local calibration policy validated by observed false-timeout rate, not an SRE-standard formula — closest published analogue AWS Agentic AI Lens 2-3×p95; 30s floor documented as cold/loaded-CI cushion); Option B size-proportional base+k×input_bytes preferred long-term; RegistryDefaults fixed `fuel_cap = 10_000_000` wrong shape for O(n)-in-input validators; D-442(e) line-count constraint is symptom not solution; Decision 5 — near-term mitigations (M2 delivered S-21.07 as a1-production-scale bats scenario fixture commit e94767bc, 576,396 bytes; M1 fuel-headroom warning pending): (M1) fuel-headroom warning on `Ok` path when `fuel_consumed > 0.9 × cap`; (M2) ≥574 KB production-scale fixture — delivered; CWE-636 defect persists until Phase 4; Decision 6 — verification requirement (Envoy #38801 lesson): behavioral tests MUST assert observed block outcome — four required cases: exhaustion+fail-closed→block; exhaustion+fail-open→advisory; `on_error` independence; crash-vs-exhaustion distinct paths; `fail_closed_timeout_with_on_error_continue_is_open` test MUST be revised not deleted when Phase 4 ships. CWE-636 primary / CWE-390 secondary. Extends ADR-035 §Decision 5. Adjudicates F-S2107-P7-010 (HIGH), F-S2107-P7-011 (HIGH), F-S2107-P7-015 (MEDIUM). **PROPOSED 2026-08-06; ADR-039 v1.0. AMENDED 2026-08-06 (v1.1 — architect): Context + Decision 3 expanded with observational evidence — PostToolUse blocks on ARCH-INDEX.md during authoring session confirm self-lock hazard is already live for today's fail-closed validators (validate-factory-path-root, validate-input-hash, validate-template-compliance, on_error=block); Phase 3-before-Phase 4 ordering constraint strengthened from precautionary to response-to-active-failure. ADR-039 v1.10.** **MECHANISM-ADJUDICATION AMENDMENT 2026-08-19 (v1.11 — architect; S-21.11 v2.0 adversarial pass-1 BLOCKER F-S2111V2-P1-001): §Decision 1's on_error/failure_policy table amendment corrected — TimeoutCause::Epoch is a GUEST-side wasmtime trap only and cannot fire while the guest is blocked inside exec_subprocess, so a legacy-bash-adapter.wasm-hosted plugin's bash-subprocess timeout never produces Timeout{cause:Epoch}; it produces a clean PluginResult::Ok{exit_code:1} (HookResult::Error) that plugin_fail_closed's Crashed\|Timeout match never catches, even under on_error="block" — this was an internal contradiction with §Decision 4's own v1.9 mechanism-precision correction, now reconciled. New §AMD-003 (PROPOSED / RATIFICATION-PENDING, POLICY 22): plugin_fail_closed (or its replacement) MUST additionally return true for on_error=Block + PluginResult::Ok{exit_code != 0} — a strict superset of the current Crashed\|Timeout rule, closing both the bash-adapter-timeout leg (F-001) and the generic HookResult::Error fail-open leg (F-005, ruled IN SCOPE for S-21.11 under the same fix per the production-grade-default principle) in one mechanism, without a new PluginResult variant. No decision semantics, threshold value, or normative prescription altered — message-string inequality symbol only; same non-decision-content erratum category as E-001..E-005 (no POLICY 22 re-ratification required). ADR-039 v1.15.** **AMD-004 + DECISION-4 TARGET-STATISTIC AMENDMENT 2026-08-22 (v1.16 — architect; D-1069, Wave-7 pre-TDD adversary pass-1 findings across S-21.20/S-21.21/S-21.22/S-21.23, three-question architect adjudication memo relayed via orchestrator, RATIFIED by human this session, POLICY 22 ratification-channel): (1) NEW §AMD-004 (RATIFIED — Q3 of the adjudication memo, S-21.23 finding F-S2123-P1-001 HIGH): §Decision 3's mandatory `break_glass.activated` Audit bullet is strengthened with a fail-closed-on-audit-failure requirement — the dispatcher's general-purpose `InternalLog::write` audit sink is deliberately best-effort (swallows I/O errors; silently no-ops on its mount-gate skip, both paths indistinguishable from a genuine write), which at the break-glass call site reproduces exactly the silent-approval state the Audit bullet forbids, one layer down. New rule: the override MUST NOT be applied unless the audit write is confirmed durably written via the new, additive `InternalLog::write_checked` tri-state method; on failure or mount-gate skip, the underlying gate's block decision stands. (2) §Decision 4 amended in place (erratum-class, no separate POLICY 22 re-ratification — Q2 of the adjudication memo, S-21.22 finding F-S2122-P1-002 HIGH): the `fuel_cap` calibration-statistic target corrected from the ambiguous `measured_p99×1.5` to the already-intended `observed_max×1.5`; the pre-existing `50_000_000` floor is now explicitly framed as an inclusive FLOOR beneath the `observed_max×1.5` TARGET, not the target itself; new `PRACTICAL_FUEL_CEILING = 500_000_000` upper-bound trigger defined (see new §Erratum E-007) — a fuel_cap larger than this ceiling signals a miscalibrated validator requiring redesign, not further headroom. Same-burst sibling propagation: BC-1.03.017 v1.19→v1.20 (Precondition 3's `fuel_cap` axis corrected + new Precondition 6 machine-checkable calibration-sufficiency gate applicable to all six §Decision 2 fail-closed validators) and BC-1.03.018 v1.1→v1.2 (new PC11 + Invariant 7 audit-durability-gates-suppression control + EC-007, implementing §AMD-004). ADR-039 v1.16.** **SEVENTH-VALIDATOR ROADMAP EXTENSION 2026-09-04 (v1.17 — architect; D-1161, S-25.04 F2 architecture decisions): §Decision 2's six-validator exhaustion-leg roadmap (validate-cross-site-correspondence, validate-factory-path-root, validate-input-hash, validate-template-compliance, validate-wave-gate-prerequisite, validate-pr-merge-prerequisites) is extended additively to a seventh entry — the new validate-factory-path-staged (BC-4.16.002, SS-04/CAP-034) PostToolUse `^Bash$` companion validator, registered under the human-ratified BROAD trigger scope (unconditional git-index/branch check on every completed Bash dispatch, no command-text pre-filter). Additive registration only — no existing Decision-2 entry's calibration status, fuel_cap, or timeout_ms changes; no re-ratification of the prior six required. Status remains RATIFIED. ADR-039 v1.17.** | SS-01, SS-02, SS-04, SS-07 | decisions/ADR-039-validator-failure-policy-resource-exhaustion-fail-closed.md |
| ADR-044 | Split-topology flip-sequencing — the enforcement-active wiring commit is capstone-owned, not core-decision-story-owned, when an atomicity-critical story is partitioned across independently-mergeable sub-stories: the commit that wires any `execute_tiers`/`execute_tier` block-decision site to consult `.failure_policy` for a `Timeout` outcome is owned by the LAST-landing story in the split (S-21.24), not the story authoring the underlying decision-function logic (S-21.19); S-21.19 authors+unit-tests the 3-arg `plugin_fail_closed` extension and `PluginOutcome.failure_policy` field as a standalone, never-wired-in-this-story pure function (inert data plumbing, does not trip PC11's live-tree detector); S-21.24's new Task 0 performs the actual wiring, strictly after all five `on_error=block` plugins are already annotated (S-21.24 `depends_on` all five sibling seams by construction); AC-002's bats fixture-registry dispatch leg and AC-011's TC-12 integration-mirror leg move with the wiring to S-21.24. Adjudicates BLOCKER F-S2119-P1-001 (S-21.19 pre-TDD adversarial pass-1: the approved 6-seam split of CONVERGED S-21.11 placed enforcement-active wiring in wave 6 while the 5 plugin annotations landed downstream in waves 7-8, opening a multi-wave CWE-636 fail-open window contradicting BC-1.03.017 v1.18 Invariant 7 + PC8/PC11's mechanical-un-mergeability guarantee). Makes "annotate before flip" hold by wave-schedule construction alone — no same-commit choreography required. Extends ADR-039 §Decision 3 (Phase 4 enforcement-flip mechanism). Full per-story change-list: `.factory/planning/S-21.11-decomposition-plan.md` §8. **RATIFIED 2026-08-20; ADR-044 v1.0.** **ADDENDUM 2026-08-22 (v1.1 — architect; D-1069, Q1 of the Wave-7 pass-1 three-question adjudication memo, S-21.21 finding F-S2121-P1-002 HIGH, RATIFIED by human this session via orchestrator relay, Option A selected): v1.0's single `plugin_fail_closed` ruling bundled TWO orthogonal predicates into one deferred-to-S-21.24 function — the `failure_policy`/`Timeout` exhaustion axis (§Decision 3 Phase 4 of ADR-039, trips PC11) and AMD-003's `on_error==Block && Ok{exit_code!=0}` error-exit axis (PC13, trips nothing in PC11) — manufacturing a false wave-8 dependency for S-21.21's own AC-013b/AC-013c end-to-end legs, which need only the error-exit axis wired live. RATIFIED split: `plugin_fail_closed_on_exhaustion(result, on_error, failure_policy)` (the exhaustion axis, governing PC1/PC2/PC3/PC5/PC6/PC10/PC11; wiring UNCHANGED, capstone-owned, still deferred to S-21.24) and `plugin_fail_closed_on_error_exit(result, on_error)` (PC13's `on_error==Block && Ok{exit_code!=0}` case; wireable immediately, now OWNED BY S-21.21 at wave 7 — requires reopening CONVERGED S-21.19 (3-CLEAN at wave 6, D-1065) to add the wiring task; the exhaustion axis's deferral to S-21.24 is UNCHANGED and remains this ADR's core v1.0 ruling). BC-1.03.017 v1.19→v1.20 Architecture Anchors sibling propagation (function-split citation) same burst. ADR-044 v1.1.** **CORRECTION 2026-08-22 (v1.2 — architect; D-1071, Wave-7 pass-2/R1 adversary round, findings F-S2119-R1-001 HIGH + F-S2121-P2-001 HIGH, independently caught by two fresh-context adversary passes): the v1.1 Addendum's item 2 read `on_error==Block && (Crashed \| Timeout)` EXTENDED to also cover `Ok{exit_code!=0}` — wording that INCLUDED `Timeout` in the error-exit leg's scope, directly contradicting that same item's own next sentence ("references neither `.failure_policy` nor `Timeout`") and BC-1.03.017 v1.20 Architecture Anchors + decomposition-plan.md §8.7, both of which scope `plugin_fail_closed_on_error_exit` to `Crashed \| Ok{exit_code!=0}` ONLY. Corrected in place: item 2 now reads `on_error==Block && Crashed` (unchanged, live today) EXTENDED to `Ok{exit_code!=0}` (PC13) — `Timeout` explicitly and unconditionally OUT OF SCOPE for this function, governed exclusively by the exhaustion leg (item 1) via `.failure_policy`. No change to item 1 or to any other ADR-044 ruling; restores internal consistency with content already ratified in the v1.1 Addendum, ADR-039, and BC-1.03.017 v1.20 — introduces no new decision, does not require separate POLICY 22 re-ratification (erratum-equivalent, same non-re-ratification category as ADR-039's E-001..E-007 precedent). ADR-044 v1.2.** **CORRECTION 2026-08-22 (v1.3 — architect; D-1073, Wave-7 pass-3 adversary round, finding F-S2121-P3-001 HIGH, verified against S-21.21): the v1.1/v1.2 Addendum's literal-replacement wording for the wave-7 error-exit wiring step (S-21.21 replaces the retained 2-arg `plugin_fail_closed` call with `plugin_fail_closed_on_error_exit`) opened a HIGH fail-open window — the retained call is the ONLY live disjunct that re-catches `Timeout` via `.failure_policy` until S-21.24's wave-8 wiring lands, so a literal replacement at wave 7 would strip `Timeout` coverage for one full wave with no disjunct catching it. Corrected: the wave-7 wiring step is now ADDITIVE — S-21.21 RETAINS the existing 2-arg `plugin_fail_closed` call and ADDS `plugin_fail_closed_on_error_exit` alongside it (OR-combined), not a replacement. S-21.24's wave-8 step gains a MIGRATION sub-task: in the SAME commit that adds `plugin_fail_closed_on_exhaustion`, REMOVE the now-redundant retained 2-arg call. New Invariant (v1.3, closes F-S2121-P3-001): at every commit from S-21.19's merge through S-21.24's merge, the live block-decision call site blocks `Timeout` under `on_error=Block` via SOME disjunct — the retained 2-arg call through wave 7, `plugin_fail_closed_on_exhaustion` from S-21.24's single migration commit onward — no commit in the range fails open on `Timeout{Fuel\|Epoch}`. No change to item 1 (exhaustion axis, still capstone-owned/S-21.24) or to the wave-8-strictly-last ordering; only the wave-7 wiring mechanism (additive, not replacing) and the wave-8 mechanism (wiring + migration, same commit) are corrected. Erratum-equivalent, same non-re-ratification category as the v1.2 correction. ADR-044 v1.3.** | SS-01 | decisions/ADR-044-split-topology-enforcement-flip-capstone-ownership.md |
| ADR-045 | Stable-anchor cross-reference architecture — eliminate load-bearing inline version pins, adopt the Doorstop fingerprint/suspect-link model, and build an AST-based residual validator to close Wave-7 version-pin convergence churn by construction: Decision 1 (proposed) — replace inline `BC vN.NN` and `ADR §Decision N vN.NN` version-pin cites in story bodies / BCs / ADRs with stable fingerprint anchors (function/invariant name, heading text) that survive spec versioning without per-cite updates; Decision 2 (proposed) — introduce a suspect-link validator (AST-based, modeled on Doorstop fingerprint/suspect-link architecture) that detects stale cites by document fingerprint comparison rather than version-string grep; Decision 3 (proposed) — establish a corpus-migration epic to retroactively replace all existing version-pin cites with stable-anchor form across Wave-7 stories, BCs, and ADRs. Root cause: four straggler failure modes observed in Wave-7 passes 4–9 (line-wrap blindness, anchor-interposed pins, live-vs-historical ambiguity, detector-regex gaps) are all variants of the same structural defect — grep-based validators cannot match patterns spanning physical lines or anchor-interposed cites. Extends ADR-039 §Decision 3. Addresses Wave-7 passes 4–9 convergence floor (D-1075–D-1081). Research grounded in `.factory/research/wave7-xref-consistency-research.md`. **PROPOSED 2026-08-24; ADR-045 v1.0. HUMAN RATIFICATION REQUIRED via POLICY 22 channel. POLICY 7/8/14/17/19 amendments deferred to ratification burst.** | SS-01, SS-04, SS-05, SS-07 | decisions/ADR-045-stable-anchor-cross-reference-architecture.md |
| ADR-047 | INDETERMINATE Outcome Model — First-Class Cannot-Complete Outcome, Durable Mutation Marker, and Next-Advance Gate: Decision 1 (trichotomy) — dispatcher outcome space expands from binary PASS/FAIL to PASS/FAIL/INDETERMINATE, where INDETERMINATE = "plugin invoked but cannot determine whether the artifact is valid" (fuel exhaustion Trap::OutOfFuel, epoch timeout Trap::Interrupt, OutputTooLarge host-sentinel + exit_code=0 + fail-closed); Decision 2 (failure_policy reuse) — INDETERMINATE behavior conditioned on existing ADR-039 `failure_policy` field: FailClosed → block + write marker; FailOpen → advisory-only, no marker; NO new `on_indeterminate` field; Decision 3 (single durable marker) — `.factory/unvalidated-mutation.marker` TOML file (fields: timestamp, plugin_name, artifact_path, cause{fuel\|epoch\|output-too-large}, trace_id) written atomically via rename; single-marker policy; Decision 4 (next-advance gate) — `validate-unvalidated-mutation-marker` PreToolUse `^Agent$` WASM plugin blocks Agent dispatch while marker exists (exit_code=2), passes when absent (exit_code=0); gate itself failure_policy=fail-open; manual `rm` escape hatch; Decision 5 (clear protocol) — marker deleted only when same plugin+artifact subsequently produces PASS; FAIL preserves marker; idempotent deletion; Decision 6 (OutputTooLarge detection) — `host_output_too_large_seen: bool` per-invocation StoreData flag; MUST be reset to false BEFORE each guest invocation (prevents N cross-contaminating N+1); checked after Ok return + exit_code=0 for FailClosed classification; Decision 7 (backward-compat) — ~76 existing fail-open plugins unaffected; canonical test `test_BC_1_18_004_fail_open_default_preserves_advisory_behavior` MUST NOT be deleted; Decision 8a (partitioned rollout) — Cohort A (Layer 1): bounded-input validators only (validate-wave-gate-prerequisite, validate-factory-path-staging, validate-cross-site-correspondence); large-artifact validators (validate-burst-log-structure, regression-gate, convergence-tracker) remain fail-open until Layer 2 sharding bounds their inputs; Decision 8b (Layer 2/3 future phases) — artifact sharding and bounded-window validation ratified as Layer 2 (S-25.02) and Layer 3 (S-25.03); Decision 9 (extended gate scope — human ratification amendment) — gate covers BOTH durable-propagation paths: (a) `^Agent$` PreToolUse dispatch (Arm 1) blocks all Agent dispatches while marker exists; (b) `^Bash$` PreToolUse dispatch matching `\bgit\b.*\b(commit\|push)\b` (Arm 2 — added by D9 human ratification amendment) blocks git commit/push Bash dispatches while marker exists; non-matching Bash dispatches (status/log/diff/fetch) proceed unconditionally; manual `rm .factory/unvalidated-mutation.marker` unblocks both arms simultaneously; no Layer 1.5 backlog story registered — Bash arm delivered in S-25.01 alongside Agent arm. BC-1.18.002 and VP-105 anchor both arms per ADR-047 §Decision 9 (v1.1 — human ratification amendment). Implementation note: distinguish fuel vs epoch via `Trap::OutOfFuel` vs `Trap::Interrupt` downcast (NOT get_fuel()==0); Trap is `#[non_exhaustive]` — wildcard match arm required. Prior art: Saltzer&Schroeder fail-safe defaults; NIST SP 800-53 SA-8(23)/(24); OWASP Fail-Securely; SMT-LIB UNKNOWN; CWE-754/400. **ACCEPTED — Human-Ratified 2026-08-30; ADR-047 v1.4 (v1.4 amendment, D-1157, 2026-09-03: TD-VSDD-060 sibling-sweep factual correction — Decision 8a's `validate-factory-path-staging` Cohort A-immediate characterization corrected from 'EFFECTIVE-NOW'/reachable-NOW to 'ASSIGNED-NOW'/CODE-reachable-unit-tested; the fail-closed bit is safely SET but produces ZERO current production enforcement effect (PreToolUse `^Bash$` registration, PostToolUse-only marker-write path per BC-1.18.001 INV4, `on_error=continue`, absent from ADR-039 §Decision 2 six-validator roadmap); routed from pr-reviewer's fresh-eyes MAJOR finding on PR #807, human-directed, NOT a design/security-model change; zero-enforcement gap anchored to new follow-up story S-25.04). PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED BY ADR-048 (D-1136; 2026-08-31): §Decision 4 fail-open-on-crash gate posture replaced by block_if_marker + marker TTL 86400s + ungated-escape invariant; §Decision 4 sub-clause only — ADR-047 trichotomy / marker / gate scope / rollout partition remain ACCEPTED.** **ERRATUM 2026-09-04 (v1.5 — architect; D-1161, S-25.04 F2 architecture elaboration): §Decision 8a's 'Known Gap' paragraph's 'artifact-write side' phrasing describing option (a)'s companion validator is corrected to point to BC-4.16.001 as validate-factory-path-staging's authoritative behavior (a narrow git-staging exclusivity guard, NOT a content validator); the Known Gap's closure is identified as the new PostToolUse detective-mirror companion validate-factory-path-staged (BC-4.16.002, SS-04/CAP-034), registered under the human-ratified BROAD trigger scope (unconditional git-index/branch check on every completed Bash PostToolUse dispatch, no command-text pre-filter). Not a POLICY 22 design/security-model change — no decision content, threshold, or normative prescription altered, consistent with ADR-039's own erratum precedent. ADR-047 v1.5.** **ADDENDUM 2026-09-05 (v1.6 — architect; S-25.02 F2 architecture-elaboration burst, co-amended alongside new ADR-051): §Decision 8a's Cohort B table plugin-name erratum corrected — the non-existent `validate-burst-log-structure` is now `validate-burst-log` (its `^(Edit\|Write\|MultiEdit)$` PostToolUse arm only; its separate `^Bash$` arm is an unrelated git-commit chain-detection gate, out of Cohort B scope). Not a POLICY 22 change — no decision content, threshold, or normative prescription altered. ADR-047 v1.6.** | SS-01, SS-03, SS-04, SS-07 | decisions/ADR-047-indeterminate-outcome-model-durable-mutation-marker-next-advance-gate.md |
| ADR-048 | Fail-Closed-But-Recoverable Gate (block_if_marker + TTL + recovery model + audited clears): Decision 1 (block_if_marker) — new on_error value; natively checks marker existence+TTL on gate-plugin crash: crash+marker+non-expired→BLOCK, crash+no-marker-or-expired→ALLOW; extends ADR-039 two-axis model with third on_error value (conditional block); replaces D-1135 fail-open-on-crash ratification; Decision 2 (marker TTL 86400s) — adds expires_at field to marker TOML (stamped at creation); TTL=86400s (24h, UNVALIDATED_MUTATION_MARKER_TTL_SECONDS in indeterminate_marker.rs); distinct from factory_lock keep-alive TTL (2700s in renew_lock_with_now); expired marker treated as absent by both plugin (normal path, auto-delete) and native crash check; backward-compat: old markers without expires_at treated as non-expired (conservative); Decision 3 (amended v1.1 — recovery model): 4-tier recovery: T1 re-validate via Edit/Write (inherently ungated; Edit/Write ≢ ^Agent$\|^Bash$; holds even through gate-plugin crash); T2 TTL deadman (no dispatch); T3 human out-of-band rm (never intercepted by PreToolUse gate — human's own shell is outside dispatcher mediation); agent-tool rm (T4) de-sanctioned — NOT a sanctioned escape; may be blocked on crash path — ACCEPTABLE because agent's T1 recovery is unaffected; shared-crate/native rm-filter rejected as unnecessary + unsound (Rice's theorem: mv/truncate/python os.remove evade any rm-specific filter; same undecidable class as BC-1.18.002 §out-of-scope); VP-107 amended to verify T1 only (Edit/Write not matched by either gate arm); recoverability invariant: T1/T2/T3 independent — no single failure mode disables all three; Decision 4 (NEW — audited clear event + TTL-loudness): marker.cleared event emitted on every clear path: clear_mode ∈ {REVALIDATED, TTL_EXPIRED, OPERATOR_OVERRIDE}; actor_type ∈ {validator, deadman, operator}; trace_id links to originating plugin.indeterminate; plugin_name, artifact_path, reason (mandatory for OPERATOR_OVERRIDE, null otherwise), timestamp; REVALIDATED emitted from delete_marker_if_pass; TTL_EXPIRED emitted from TTL auto-delete branch (TTL-loudness — previously silent); OPERATOR_OVERRIDE reconciled retroactively via RAW_DELETE_DETECTED (marker absent + unmatched plugin.indeterminate → infer operator out-of-band rm; best-effort FileSink read); emission via FileSink/InternalLog; ninth dispatcher domain event (BC-3.08.001 enumeration seven→eight→nine; PO + test-writer downstream sweep REQUIRED); proportionality: no signed digests/dual-control — cooperating-agent threat model; GitHub branch-protection + factory-artifacts worktree provide VCS-layer tamper-evidence. Prior art: NIST AU-3/AU-9/AU-10, PCI-DSS 10.2, circuit-breaker reset, EDR Restore, Google break-glass, LOTO/e-stop. **Decision 4 v1.2 amendment (S-25.01 LOCAL adversary pass 2 F-P2-002 HIGH + F-P2-003 MED):** TTL_EXPIRED and OPERATOR_OVERRIDE emission moved from the WASM gate plugin to dispatcher-native code (new `check_and_clear_expired_marker` + `reconcile_raw_delete` in indeterminate_marker.rs), mirroring the already-correct REVALIDATED architecture — the `emit_event` host ABI's RESERVED_FIELDS enrichment makes it structurally impossible for a WASM plugin to emit an event carrying a foreign (marker-owned) trace_id/plugin_name; `evaluate_gate` (WASM) is simplified to a pure marker-presence check with no TTL logic. Supersedes ADR-047 §Decision 4 (fail-open-on-crash gate posture) + D-1135 (fail-open ratification). **Decision 4 v1.3 amendment (S-25.01 LOCAL adversary pass 3 F-P3-001 MEDIUM + F-P3-002 LOW):** emission-mechanism precision correction — Events 8/9 emission (`emit_indeterminate`, `emit_marker_cleared`, `check_and_clear_expired_marker`, `reconcile_raw_delete`) now routes through `HostContext::emit_internal` (the same dual-sink helper every sibling BC-3.08.001 dispatcher-native event already uses), not a raw `InternalLog::write`; `reconcile_raw_delete`'s bounded scan target corrected to `dispatcher-internal-{date}.jsonl` (the only sink unconditionally durable in every production dispatcher run) — not a literal `events-{date}.jsonl`, pending S-4.07's Router/FileSink wiring; new fourth `clear_mode = "SUPERSEDED"` / `actor_type = "system"` closes the cross-pair marker-overwrite false-`OPERATOR_OVERRIDE`-attribution gap. **Decision 4 v1.4 amendment (S-25.01 adversary pass 6 F-P6-001 MEDIUM):** reconciliation-premise correction — new dispatcher-native audit event `marker.written` (BC-3.08.001 Event 10), emitted by `write_indeterminate_marker`'s caller ONLY immediately after the atomic write returns `Ok(())`, never before, never on write failure; `reconcile_raw_delete`'s OPERATOR_OVERRIDE scan retargets from unmatched `plugin.indeterminate` (unsound — fires for every INDETERMINATE outcome regardless of whether a marker was ever written) to unmatched `marker.written` (sound by construction), closing a false-attribution gap reachable via a PreToolUse fail-closed INDETERMINATE (marker write is PostToolUse-only) or a PostToolUse marker-write I/O failure. **Decision 4 v1.5 amendment (S-25.01 adversary pass 9 F-P9-001 MEDIUM):** SUPERSEDED emission-point correction, symmetric with the v1.4 marker.written fix — `emit_superseded_if_cross_pair` was emitting `marker.cleared(SUPERSEDED, system)` for the OLD pair UNCONDITIONALLY, BEFORE `write_indeterminate_marker` attempted the overwrite it describes; if that write then returned `Err(_)` (EC-007, swallowed best-effort), the OLD marker was still durably present and still enforcing, yet a SUPERSEDED record had already falsely claimed it was overwritten — a fabricated audit record (NIST AU-3/AU-10), the un-swept sibling of the v1.4 `marker.written` "emit only after `Ok(())`" discipline. Fix: `emit_superseded_if_cross_pair`'s call relocates to inside `write_indeterminate_marker`'s `Ok(())` arm, alongside the (unchanged) `emit_marker_written` call; on `Err(_)`, NEITHER event is emitted. No new event, field, or `clear_mode` value — emission-point fix only; closes the emit-after-success class for §Decision 4 (only two write-tied events exist: `marker.written` and `marker.cleared(SUPERSEDED)`, both now governed by the identical rule). **ACCEPTED — Human-Ratified 2026-09-01** (POLICY 22; ADR-048 v1.6, amended 2026-09-01/2026-09-03 — v1.1 HIGH-1 resolution; v1.2 emission-point correction (D-1139); v1.3 emission-mechanism precision correction + SUPERSEDED clear_mode (D-1140); v1.4 reconciliation-premise correction (D-1141); v1.5 SUPERSEDED emission-point correction (D-1142); v1.6 TD-VSDD-060 sibling-sweep factual correction (D-1157, 2026-09-03) — 5 loci that mislabeled `validate-factory-path-staging`'s Cohort A-immediate assignment as EFFECTIVE-NOW/reachable-NOW corrected to ASSIGNED-NOW/CODE-reachable-unit-tested (frontmatter historical Prior text, the Decision-4-v1.4-origin body paragraph, the Decision 4 v1.4 section, the Consequences section, and the Adjudication History v1.4-origin entry); new Adjudication History entry anchors the zero-enforcement gap to follow-up story S-25.04; pure factual correction, NOT a POLICY 22/design-substance change — INDETERMINATE classification and Event 8 emission remain independent of `on_error` per ADR-039 §Decision 1 axes-independence). | SS-01, SS-04, SS-07 | decisions/ADR-048-fail-closed-but-recoverable-gate-block-if-marker-crash-policy-marker-ttl-deadman-and-ungated-escape-invariant.md |
| ADR-049 | `last_amended` Write-Path Durable Fix — Current-Entry-Only Scalar Plus Existing `changelog:` Sequence: S-15.03 Phase A design + AC-002 validator-compatibility audit for the D-1149 durable-fix follow-up. Audit findings: (1) F-P4-004 block-scalar-indicator bug is already fixed in `frontmatter.rs::collect_block_scalar_body`; (2) Class E1/E2 (`arm_e`) never fires on any of the 5 target index/state files — `dispatch.rs::is_frontmatter_parity_target` explicitly excludes BC-INDEX.md/STORY-INDEX.md/VP-INDEX.md and never admits ARCH-INDEX.md/STATE.md; (3) ARCH-INDEX.md/BC-INDEX.md/VP-INDEX.md already carry a live `changelog:` sequence, inert to every current reader; (4) count-propagation and changelog-monotonicity hooks are unaffected; (5) validate-state-structure and verify-factory-lock are unaffected; (6) `compute-input-hash` hashes full file content (not `last_amended` specifically) — the one-time migration requires a standard `--update`/`--scan` resync for dependents. **ACCEPTED — Human-Ratified 2026-09-02 (POLICY 22).** Decision: `last_amended:` becomes CURRENT-ENTRY-ONLY (single-line quoted scalar, never bracket-wrapped) on all 5 files; the outgoing entry is prepended to `changelog:` (ARCH-INDEX/BC-INDEX/VP-INDEX today; STORY-INDEX.md gains `changelog:`, completing the D-448(b)/D-414(c) deferral; STATE.md relies on its existing body-level `## Decisions Log`/`## Phase Progress` instead of a frontmatter `changelog:`); D-1149 sidecars FROZEN (not folded back); AC-006 migration/rotation tool is a Rust `bin/` binary (POLICY 21) that also remediates the D-1144 unescaped-quote defect on BC-INDEX.md/ARCH-INDEX.md/STATE.md in the same pass. Selects S-15.03 Option 2 (`changelog:` sequence) over Option 1 (block scalar on `last_amended` itself, also found viable but higher surface area) and over stop-accumulating. | SS-04, SS-05, SS-06, SS-10 | decisions/ADR-049-last-amended-write-path-durable-fix-current-entry-plus-changelog-sequence.md |
| ADR-050 | CI Darwin-Leg Source-Build Discipline for Registry-Schema Forward Compatibility: `bats-darwin-leg-macos` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) currently copies the COMMITTED, release-cadence-stale bundled darwin binary for its T-001 dispatcher setup step, instead of a PR-source-built one — any PR that adds a new registry-schema `on_error`/`failure_policy` enum variant (e.g. S-25.01's ADR-048 §Decision 1 `block_if_marker`) alongside its first consuming registry entry deterministically fails this leg (`unknown variant` TOML parse error), because the stale binary predates the new variant and `OnError`'s `Deserialize` has no `#[serde(other)]` fallback. Decision 1 — `bats-darwin-leg-macos` gains `needs: build-dispatcher` and downloads the ALREADY-BUILT, currently-unconsumed `factory-dispatcher-darwin-{arm64,x64}` `actions/upload-artifact` produced by `ci.yml`'s existing `build-dispatcher` matrix job (built from THIS PR's own source, zero extra CI compute) instead of copying `plugins/vsdd-factory/hooks/dispatcher/bin/darwin-*/factory-dispatcher`. Decision 2 — a separate, narrow "bundle smoke-test" step (`--version` on the COMMITTED binary, asserting clean exit) is added to preserve a minimal committed-artifact-integrity signal without coupling it to registry-schema compatibility. Decision 3 — establishes the general forward-compatibility principle that future PRs may land a new registry-schema value AND its first consuming registry entry in the SAME PR with no interim/staged/weakened value and no two-phase landing choreography, since CI now always validates a PR's own freshly-built dispatcher against its own registry. Explicitly rejects staging the two new S-25.01 registry entries at an already-supported interim `on_error` value (`"continue"` reproduces the exact D-1135 fail-open-on-crash CWE-636 gap ADR-048 was human-directed to reverse; `"block"` reproduces the unconditional self-lock posture ADR-048's own Alternatives Considered section already rejected) — both interim options force a security-posture compromise this CI-sequencing fix avoids entirely. Confirmed via direct inspection that `release.yml`'s "Pre-release Validation" job runs `ubuntu-latest` only — no macOS/bash-3.2 validation exists anywhere in the release pipeline today, so `bats-darwin-leg-macos` is currently the only (and, per its own header comments, incidentally rather than by design) exerciser of the committed darwin bundle. **ACCEPTED — Human-Ratified 2026-09-03 (POLICY 22; D-1158); architect determination per `determination-S2501-block-if-marker-bundle-sequencing.md`; implementation routed to devops-engineer, landing concurrently on `feature/S-25.01` to unblock PR #807's `bats-darwin-leg-macos` CI failure.** | SS-01, SS-07 | decisions/ADR-050-ci-darwin-leg-source-build-discipline-for-registry-schema-forward-compatibility.md |
| ADR-051 | Layer-2 Two-Mechanism Size-Triggered Shard Rotation — Cycle Append-Logs and BC-INDEX Structured-Catalog Sharding: per D-1166 human widest-scope decision, S-25.02 covers BOTH (A) a new native (non-WASM) PreToolUse shard-cap gate in the dispatcher (`crates/factory-dispatcher/src/shard_manager.rs`, no new crate, no HOST_ABI_VERSION bump) rotating the four cycle append-logs (decision-log.md, burst-log.md, lessons.md, session-checkpoints.md) via stable-current-filename addressing + block-and-retry roll-before-write (resolving the structural gap that `HookResult`'s Continue/Block/Error-only contract cannot transparently redirect writes), AND (B) BC-INDEX.md two-level structured-catalog sharding — B1 (frontmatter `changelog:` array rotation reusing the already-shipped `rotate_changelog`, `crates/last-amended-migrate/src/rotate.rs`, per ADR-049 §Decision 6) plus B2 (per-subsystem `### SS-NN` body-table sharding addressed via the existing BC-S-Prefix→SS-NN mapping, no new addressing scheme). Shard-cap formula `shard_cap_bytes <= (PRACTICAL_FUEL_CEILING / WORST_CASE_FUEL_PER_BYTE) - MAX_SINGLE_RECORD_BYTES - SAFETY_MARGIN` calibrated via a synthetic harness (owned by performance-engineer at F4) grounded in ADR-042's measured 53.18 fuel/byte linear model; provisional illustrative cap ~49,152 bytes at today's 10M-effective DEFAULT_FUEL_CAP. Identifies a mandatory one-time backfill-split of the four already-oversized append-log files (5–19× over the illustrative cap) as a required BC-authorship input, not previously in the story draft. Resolves OQ-2 (two different addressing mechanisms for the two artifact shapes), OQ-3 (`/compact-state` gets shard-awareness for free via the native gate), OQ-4 (synthetic harness + provisional constants), OQ-5 (co-amended into ADR-047 v1.5→v1.6 same burst — corrected non-existent `validate-burst-log-structure` to actual `validate-burst-log`). Assessed and REJECTED a split-to-follow-up-story proposal for BC-INDEX sharding (kept fully in scope) because B1/B2 both reuse existing primitives/addressing schemes rather than inventing new architecture. **FIX-BURST AMENDMENT 2026-09-05 (v1.1 — architect; fresh-context adversary pass-1 findings against the authored BC-1.18.005–010/BC-7.08.001 set):** §Decision 7's B1 sub-mechanism CORRECTED from a double-actor "gate rotates+prepends the new item, then Continue" design (unsound — double-prepend/stale-payload-clobber, verified against `rotate_changelog`'s actual pure-trim signature) to a single-actor block-and-retry contract structurally identical to mechanism A's (gate performs ONLY the trim, then Blocks with a retry instruction; the landing agent call performs the sole prepend) [F-S2502-F2-001, BLOCKER]. NEW §Decision 10 added: a governed one-time migration for the B2 BC-INDEX body split (content-preservation, independent-census, atomicity, rollback), closing a gap parallel to BC-1.18.008's mechanism-A backfill [F-S2502-F2-002, HIGH]. §Decision 1 amended with explicit trigger-shape dispatch (BC-1.18.005 owns both the byte-size and item-count trigger shapes) [F-S2502-F2-005]. §Decision 6 amended with a code-grounded whole-corpus history-scanning validator enumeration and a POLICY-1/archival reconciliation [F-S2502-F2-008]. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — corrects/completes v1.0's own stated intent. **FIX-BURST AMENDMENT 2026-09-05 (v1.2 — architect; fresh-context adversary pass-2 findings against the authored BC-1.18.005/006/009/011 set, grounded in direct re-inspection of `rotate_changelog`'s shipped implementation):** §Decision 7's B1 archive scheme CORRECTED (an impossible per-`seq` sealed-shard-directory layout replaced with the single evergreen append-file the shipped function actually produces, via a bounded named path-resolution extension) [F-P2-001, HIGH]. §Decision 1 step 3 CORRECTED (tool-discriminated `projected_size` — `Write`: `len(content)` alone; `Edit`/`MultiEdit`: unchanged current+delta) [F-P2-002, HIGH]. §Decision 3 CORRECTED (seal changed from rename-away, which opened an ENOENT window and contradicted BC-1.18.006's own Invariant 3, to copy-then-atomic-truncate-in-place; retry wording unified) [F-P2-003, HIGH]. NEW §Decision 11 (staged, crash-recoverable per-write roll; new error codes E-SHD-006/E-SHD-007) [F-P2-004, MEDIUM]. NEW §Decision 12 (append-only-tail assumption explicit; sealed-shard direct-edit escape hatch) [F-P2-005, MEDIUM]. NEW §Decision 13 (governed one-time B1 changelog backfill migration required, illustratively BC-1.18.012, modeled on BC-1.18.008) [F-P2-007, MEDIUM]. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — corrects/completes v1.1's own stated intent against the actual shipped code. **FIX-BURST AMENDMENT 2026-09-05 (v1.3 — architect; fresh-context adversary pass-3 findings against the authored BC-1.18.005/009/012 set):** NEW §Decision 14 added — high-water/low-water hysteresis for B1's item-count rotation target, closing a steady-state defect where trimming to exactly `N-1` on every rotation left the live sequence at the trigger boundary after the very next write, causing every subsequent write to `BC-INDEX.md` to pay a block+retry round-trip; a new sibling config value `low_water_mark` (default `floor(N/2)`) becomes the rotation target instead of `N-1`, reusing `rotate_changelog`'s already-free `keep_recent` parameter (zero new rotation logic); mechanism A confirmed to need no analogous change (its seal already truncates to fully empty on every roll) [F-P3-005, MEDIUM]. §Decision 13 amended in the same burst so the one-time cold-start backfill migration targets the same `low_water_mark`, not `N` (a consistency propagation caught proactively). §Decision 10 corrected (stale illustrative `total_bcs` citation re-grounded to a structural, count-redacted description per TD-VSDD-091) [F-P3-007, LOW]. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — corrects/completes v1.2's own stated intent. **FIX-BURST AMENDMENT 2026-09-06 (v1.4 — architect; fresh-context adversary pass-4 findings against the authored BC-1.18.005/009/012 + VP-140/VP-125 set):** §Decision 14 CORRECTED (F-P4-001, HIGH, ADJUDICATION) — `low_water_mark = N-1` was simultaneously "VALID" (BC-1.18.005's own Canonical Test Vectors, VP-140, VP-125) and "fail-loud malformed" (Decision 14's own v1.3 prose, BC-1.18.005 EC-011), a live self-contradiction since `N-1 < N` mathematically satisfies the declared `0 <= low_water_mark < N` constraint; ADJUDICATED as Option (b) — the constraint is unchanged and correct, the erroneous "fail-loud on N-1" prose is withdrawn, and the latent amortization-pathology concern is closed via a NEW non-fatal `tracing::warn!` advisory (fires when `low_water_mark > floor(N/2)`) instead of a false fail-loud claim. §Decision 13 CORRECTED (F-P4-004, LOW) — stale worked-example arithmetic `1,947` corrected to `1,972`, matching BC-1.18.012 v1.1's own EC-001/CTV figures. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — corrects/completes v1.3's own stated intent. **FIX-BURST AMENDMENT 2026-09-06 (v1.5 — architect; S-25.02 F2 gate-audit finding F3, MEDIUM, ADJUDICATION, ahead of the F2 human ratification gate):** `subsystems_affected`'s SS-04 entry carried no substantive body justification; ADJUDICATED Option (a) — genuinely affected, since §Decision 6 audits four SS-04-owned WASM crates (`validate-dispatch-advance`, `validate-state-structure`, `validate-closes-completeness`, `validate-cross-site-correspondence`) for archival-scheme correctness and imposes a new archive-inclusive-glob obligation on POLICY-1's SS-04-adjacent enforcement path. Added an explicit one-line justification note at the top of §Decision 6; the Subsystems column below is UNCHANGED (already correct, only unjustified). CAP-043's SS-01/SS-07-only subsystem list flagged as a product-owner follow-up, not amended here. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — justification only, no decision content altered. **FIX-BURST AMENDMENT 2026-09-06 (v1.6 — architect; S-25.02 F2 gate-audit fix, F-P6-001, MEDIUM):** §Decision 6's opening justification paragraph carried a stale present-tense claim that CAP-043's SS-01/SS-07-only subsystem list was "incomplete"/"a product-owner follow-up not amended here"; that follow-up CLOSED same-cycle (CAP-043 v1.21 now lists SS-01/SS-04/SS-07) — rewrote the clause past-tense/closure-acknowledging, referencing CAP-043's §Subsystems list structurally per TD-VSDD-091. SS-04 justification substance unchanged. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — stale cross-reference correction only. **FIX-BURST AMENDMENT 2026-09-06 (v1.7 — architect; S-25.02 F2 sibling-sweep micro-burst, adversary pass-7 F-P7-001 closure):** four LIVE occurrences (§Decision 7's pure-trim descriptor + step 3/4 citations, §Decision 11's staged-roll-sequence heading, the Rationale section's pure-trim descriptor) still described B1's rotation target as a literal `N-1`, contradicting §Decision 14 (v1.3+), which replaced the fixed `N-1` eviction target with the configured `low_water_mark` (default `floor(N/2)`) precisely to close the every-write rotation-churn pathology §Decision 14 documents; corrected all four to cite `low_water_mark`/`keep_recent` generically, each with an explicit "NEVER a fixed `N-1`" cross-reference to §Decision 14 — no decision content changed, exposition brought into agreement with the ADR's own already-adopted §Decision 14. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal. **POLICY 22 STATUS FLIP 2026-09-06 (v1.8 — architect; D-1167, S-25.02 Phase F2 CLOSE):** human REVIEWED the full spec (including the simpler/validator-fix alternatives) and RATIFIED the current two-mechanism shard-rotation design as-is; status `proposed` -> `accepted`. ADJUDICATED against the top-of-file BROWNFIELD template note (implementation-evidence-before-acceptance): this ADR is forward design for a not-yet-built module (`shard_manager.rs`, the `rotate_changelog` `archive_path` extension, both F4-implementer scope) and already cites file:line evidence for every REUSED existing primitive (`write_atomic`, `write_indeterminate_marker`/`block_if_marker_check`, `HookResult`, `rotate_changelog`/`resolve_archive_path`); followed the SAME precedent this project already set for identically-postured forward-design ADRs carrying the identical BROWNFIELD comment — ADR-048 and ADR-049 (both `status: accepted` with the comment still present, new-code implementation evidence delivered downstream, not at the acceptance gate) and ADR-050/ADR-039's AMD-NNN sub-decisions (POLICY 22 ratification flips status purely on human sign-off of the DESIGN, with implementation explicitly ROUTED AFTER acceptance — ADR-050's own v4.13 ARCH-INDEX row: "ci.yml implementation routed to devops-engineer" post-flip) confirm the same pattern platform-wide. New-module implementation evidence (`shard_manager.rs` file:line, the `resolve_archive_path`-generalization diff) is deferred to F4 per this ADR's own §Decision 2/4/7/11/13 F4-ownership language — an explicit, named future-story dependency (S-25.02 F4), not an unattached defer. **ADDENDUM 2026-09-07 (v1.9 — architect; ARCH-citation-gap closure, S-25.02 cluster-2):** NEW §Decision 15 — PostToolUse Native Reconciliation Leg for BC-1.18.006 Postcondition 7 (`replace_all` closure); documents the PostToolUse-side native-check leg BC-1.18.006 v1.4's Postcondition 7 catch point (i) requires, closing an overclaim in BC-1.18.006's own Traceability section ("No NEW ADR decision was required... contained within ADR-051's existing native-check... pattern") — §Decision 1 as originally written scopes its native-check pattern strictly to the top of the dispatcher's PreToolUse handling and to a Continue/Block/Error-signaling check; it has no PostToolUse leg and no provision for a silent, non-signaling check. §Decision 15 extends §Decision 1's pattern to a PostToolUse call site (catch point (i)); establishes that this leg emits NO `HookResult` (a pure filesystem seal+truncate side effect, per BC-1.18.006 EC-016), contrasting with §Decision 1's own signaling PreToolUse leg; specifies retroactive reuse of Postcondition 1's/§Decision 11's existing four-step roll sequence against already-on-disk content (no new roll logic, no new error code); and states a placement caveat — the check must be an unconditional native call inside `factory_dispatcher::main::run` BEFORE its `sync_tiers.is_empty() && partition.async_group.is_empty()` early-return guard, mirroring §Decision 1's own "before the registry-driven plugin loop" rule, so the leg cannot silently stop firing if the registered PostToolUse plugin set changes — grounded against `write_indeterminate_marker` (`indeterminate_marker.rs`, invoked from `executor.rs`) and the `git_context` injection (`inject_git_context_if_qualifying`, ADR-029 §Decision 1-3) as existing native-call-in-`run` precedents. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — no existing Decision's content is altered; this is a net-new addendum closing a citation gap identified during architect review of BC-1.18.006 v1.4. Catch point (ii) (the next-dispatch backstop) remains governed by §Decision 1's existing PreToolUse scope. **ADDENDUM 2026-09-07 (v1.10 — architect; S-25.02 cluster-2 LOCAL adversary pass-4, closing an ADR-level gap BC-1.18.006 v1.8's F-C2-P4-001/F-C2-P4-002 findings flagged):** NEW §Decision 16 — resolves F-C2-P4-001 (MAJOR, data-loss) at the ADR level: §Decision 15's PostToolUse reconciliation leg was silent on self-heal call order; a reachable counterexample (a prior roll crashes as `E-SHD-006`, leaving the shard-index EMPTY; the next matched dispatch is a content-changing `replace_all` `Edit` pushing the canonical over cap) shows catch point (i)'s seal-publish step, without a self-heal pre-pass, computes `next_seal_seq` from the UNRECONCILED index and OVERWRITES a durably-sealed prior shard. §Decision 16 item 1 corrects §Decision 15's silence — catch point (i) MUST run `run_self_heal_if_plausible` BEFORE calling `execute_roll`, matching the PreToolUse Flat arm's existing call order exactly (a REQUIRED symmetry, not an intentional asymmetry); catch point (ii) is unaffected (already self-heal-protected). §Decision 16 item 2 resolves F-C2-P4-002 (MINOR, defense-in-depth): `publish_sealed_shard` becomes write-once — before every seal-publish rename, the gate verifies the destination does not already exist; on collision it returns `HookResult::Error` (NEW `E-SHD-009`, "sealed-shard immutability violation") and applies neither the seal nor any subsequent roll step, layered ON TOP OF item 1's ordering fix, not a substitute for it. No new crate, no `HOST_ABI_VERSION` bump, no new `hooks-registry.toml` entry, no new `HookResult` variant beyond the existing `Error` arm. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — no existing Decision's content is altered; a net-new addendum, mirroring §Decision 15's own precedent. Downstream propagation (VP-119 write-once facet, VP-INDEX pending VP-NNN row EC-023 facet, verification-architecture.md/verification-coverage-matrix.md) is this same burst's separate, concurrent architect task (POLICY 9). **ADDENDUM 2026-09-08 (v1.11 — architect; closes PR #824 cycle-3 review findings MAJOR-2 and MAJOR-3 against BC-1.18.006 v1.11's cluster-2 delivery):** NEW §Decision 17 — PreToolUse shard-cap gate hoisted above the early-return guard; catch point (i) gains entry validation. MAJOR-3: the `main.rs` placement-rationale comment's PreToolUse "plugin-set-independence" claim was false — `executor::shard_cap_precheck` was invoked from inside `executor::execute_tiers`, which `main::run` skips entirely whenever its `sync_tiers.is_empty() && partition.async_group.is_empty()` early-return guard fires on an empty matched-plugin set, silently defeating the gate; resolved by hoisting the gate's check to `main::run`, before that guard, alongside catch point (i)'s existing call site (Decision 17 item 1). MAJOR-2: catch point (i) reached the destructive `execute_roll` via `find_matching_entry` alone, without `shard_manager::validate_entry`, bypassing EC-022 and the EC-010/011/013/015/017 cap-sanity family; resolved by wiring `validate_entry` into `invoke::detect_replace_all_overcap_candidate` immediately after `find_matching_entry`, matching Decision 1's own leg (Decision 17 item 2). No new crate, no `HOST_ABI_VERSION` bump, no new `hooks-registry.toml` entry, no new `HookResult` variant. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — neither correction alters existing Decision content (Decision 1's and Decision 15's own text were silent/ambiguous, not wrong); a net-new addendum per this ADR's established addendum convention (Decisions 11-16). Downstream: implementer wires both fixes plus two regression tests (Decision 17 items 4-5); product-owner propagates the corrected invocation-point citation into BC-1.18.005's Architecture Anchors (v1.14→v1.15) and BC-1.18.006's Traceability ADR row (v1.11→v1.12). **ADDENDUM 2026-09-11 (v1.12 — architect; S-25.02 cluster-4 hardening, closes EC-008/Inv-5 and Inv-6 gaps in §Decision 7 B1):** §Decision 7 B1 subsection amended with two cluster-4 hardening bullets: (1) Obs A guard-arm completeness (EC-008/Inv-5) — when `rotate_changelog_at` returns `Ok(report)` with `report.mutated==false` (item-count trigger fires but archive write is a no-op because the byte-level tail-match dedup guard already saw that content), the B1 gate MUST return `HookResult::Error(E-SHD-014)`, NEVER `HookResult::Block`; a Block would re-enter the trigger check on the very next write, reaching the same `Ok(mutated=false)` outcome indefinitely — a verified infinite block+retry self-DoS. (2) Obs B crash-idempotency (Inv-6) — `rotate_changelog_at`'s idempotent-append guarantees the evergreen archive NEVER accumulates duplicate items across a crash-and-retry boundary: the byte-level tail-match dedup test at the archive file's suffix means any retry of a partially-completed append correctly identifies the already-committed suffix and skips the write, making the B1 archive boundary crash-safe with zero explicit recovery logic. Not a POLICY 22 design-direction reversal — both bullets close hardening gaps identified by adversarial review of cluster-4; no existing Decision content altered. Downstream propagation: VP-126 v1.4/VP-131 v1.4/VP-112 v1.1 (POLICY 9, same burst). **ACCEPTED — Human-Ratified 2026-09-06 (D-1167); ADR-051 v1.12.** | SS-01, SS-04, SS-07 | decisions/ADR-051-layer-2-two-mechanism-size-triggered-shard-rotation-append-logs-and-bc-index-sharding.md |
| ADR-046 | PostToolUse hook-authored STATE.md wall-clock stamping (timestamp + lock keep-alive) — move `timestamp:` and `factory_lock.expires_at` from agent-authored to hook-authored so agents never manage them and cannot get them wrong: Decision 1 — new native WASM PostToolUse plugin `stamp-state-timestamp` (SS-04) that unconditionally re-stamps `timestamp:` to `chrono::Utc::now()` on every qualifying Edit/Write/MultiEdit to `.factory/STATE.md`, and conditionally renews `factory_lock.expires_at` to `now + TTL_SECONDS` (the same non-configurable 2700s constant sourced from `factory-lock-write.sh`/BC-5.40.001 Invariant 2, not reinvented) — gated on the writer's resolved git identity matching the recorded `factory_lock.holder` (mirrors `verify-factory-lock`'s existing self-held comparison; closes a holder-identity gap present in today's manual renewal flow, where neither `factory-lock-write.sh renew` nor the guard being retired checks who is renewing); Decision 2 — retire (deregister, not merely relax) `verify-state-timestamp-refresh` in full, since its two enforcement arms (timestamp staleness, lock-expiry staleness) become structurally unreachable once the stamper ships and it has no third function; Decision 3 — both halves ship in one release, never split. Scope boundary: lock acquire/release/CAS-push semantics stay with `factory-lock-write.sh`/the agent — only the mid-burst keep-alive renewal moves to the hook. Proposes companion amendments to BC-5.40.001 (PC4 actor reassignment) and a new BC-4.17.001 for the plugin; confirms BC-6.23.001 (acquire/release only) is unaffected. **RATIFIED 2026-08-25; current version per ADR-046 frontmatter (tail records bump history).** BC-5.39.001 3-CLEAN spec-convergence gate IN PROGRESS since ratification: 24 passes run, 6 genuine findings fixed (F-P10-001/F-P13-001/F-P15-001/F-P18-001 HIGH, F-P21-001/F-P23-001 MED), streak reached 1/3 at pass-24. **Pass-25 (2026-08-26) FINDINGS (2), both fixed, streak RESET 1/3→0/3:** F-P25-001 MED — Invariant/§Decision 1/File-Change Plan corrected: `renew_lock_if_holder`'s own independent `flp::parse_factory_lock(content)` parse at the holder-present step yields a `LockState` (crate `factory-lock-parse`), NOT a `FactoryLock` (the distinct struct in crate `factory-lock`, produced only by `factory_lock::parse_lock`) — closes the O-P24-001 LOW type-provenance nit tracked since pass-24, now escalated and RESOLVED. F-P25-002 MED — File-Change Plan's implementing-story anchor resolved from "[pending]"/S-17.05-unlisted to the confirmed story S-17.05 (`stamp-state-timestamp-hook`, E-17 Wave 5, 8pts, `tdd_mode: strict`), consistent across ADR-046 and both companion BCs (BC-4.17.001, BC-7.07.001). **Pass-26 (2026-08-26) FINDINGS (1) MED, fixed, streak REMAINS 0/3 (already reset at pass-25 — this finding does not reset an already-0/3 streak further):** F-P26-001 MED (POLICY 14/17/6) — the File-Change Plan's own ARCH-INDEX sync-instruction row (this very row) had drifted stale, still directing a bump to "v1.10"/pass-21 after the pass-25 architect edit had already advanced the ADR to v1.11 — a sibling-instruction-row sweep gap (TD-VSDD-060-class), not a defect in the row's history content; resolved by rewriting the row to direct v1.12, self-consistent with the version this revision produces. 2 LOW observations recorded non-blocking: O-P26-001 (BC-7.07.001 `status:active` carries not-yet-implemented ADR-046 amendment invariants — judged WORKING-AS-DESIGNED spec-leading-code per S-17.05 anchor, no BC edit) and O-P26-002 (`[process-gap]` SS-07 "Hook Bash Layer" label an increasing misnomer as native-WASM hook plugins accrete — deferred, anchored future ARCH-INDEX subsystem-label review). **Pass-27 (2026-08-26): ADR-046 UNCHANGED — VERDICT FINDINGS (3), all BC-only (F-P27-001 HIGH §Story Anchor sibling-sweep gap, F-P27-002/F-P27-003 MED status/lifecycle + inputs: completeness, all fixed same-burst on BC-5.40.001/BC-7.07.001); no ADR-046 touch this pass, row not bumped.** **Pass-28 (2026-08-26) FINDINGS (2: 1 HIGH + 1 MED), both fixed, streak REMAINS 0/3 (already reset at pass-25 — these findings do not reset an already-0/3 streak further):** F-P28-001 HIGH (POLICY 18) — ADR-046's own `inputs:` omitted `crates/factory-lock-parse/src/lib.rs` despite the entire F-P25-001/v1.11 correction resting on that crate, and omitted `.factory/specs/behavioral-contracts/ss-07/BC-7.07.001.md` despite it already being named in this ADR's own Source/Origin § alongside three sibling BCs that ARE in `inputs:`; | ADR-040 | POLICY 15 ATTESTATION-LOCATION GATE — parent-SHA predicate replaces self-referential HEAD-SHA: Decision 1 — D-912 HEAD-SHA predicate is logically unsatisfiable (git commit SHA = hash(tree_including_attestation_section) → circular; predicate has returned 0 at every HEAD since D-912 and can never return 1); Decision 2 — new predicate: `PARENT=$(git -C <factory-worktree-root> rev-parse HEAD^1) && grep -c "assertion-site attestation ($PARENT)" <red-gate-log-path>` → 1; attestation heading form `### Pass-N assertion-site attestation (<PARENT-SHA>)` replaces D-912 `### Pass-N assertion-site attestation (<HEAD-SHA>)`; parent SHA known at authoring time (not circular); Decision 3 — TD-VSDD-053 interaction: same-commit bundling retained unchanged — state-manager writes both assertion-site changes and `### Pass-N assertion-site attestation (<PARENT-SHA>)` section in one commit; parent SHA available as `git rev-parse HEAD^1` before staging, no circular dependency; Decision 4 — mechanization verdict: predicate is fully mechanizable as PreToolUse hook or CI step (literal shell script in §Decisions); MUST be invoked with captured stdout per D-449(a)/META-LEVEL-24; narrative attestation forbidden; Decision 5 — sibling-sweep (TD-VSDD-060): literal shell sweep of policies.yaml for `HEAD-SHA\|actual SHA being pushed` yields two matches both in the same POLICY 15 ATTESTATION-LOCATION GATE bullet; F-S2107-P8-003 is an instance, not a class; no other verification_steps predicate embeds a commit's own SHA. Supersedes POLICY 15 ATTESTATION-LOCATION GATE clause codified at D-912 (D-912's POLICY 13 mutant-derived-gate alternation mandate is unaffected). Adjudicates F-S2107-P8-003 (HIGH [process-gap]). policies.yaml NOT yet edited — **human ratification required**. **PROPOSED 2026-08-07; ADR-040 v1.0. No `GateOutcome` variant, exit code, or predicate semantics changed. ADR-040 v1.17. AMENDED 2026-08-15 (v1.18 — architect; PR #777 cognitive-diversity code-reviewer CR-2 adjudication + three EXECUTION-based pr-reviewer findings against PR #777 HEAD `010e6140`): CR-2 (MEDIUM) STRUCTURAL REFACTOR directed — new `GateResult { outcome, skipped_parentless }` wrapper replaces bare `GateOutcome` on `run_gate`/`run_gate_from_merge_base`/`run_gate_inner`; the sole `eprintln!` moves from `src/lib.rs` into `main.rs`, restoring exhaustive-match discoverability. **H-1 [HIGH, ACCEPTED as genuine gate defect]** — merge commits (parent-count > 1) false-FAIL under the two-dot diff, which re-flags already-attested second-parent content as unattested; new Ruling 9(e) evaluates merge commits via git's COMBINED diff (`git diff-tree -c --name-only`) instead, with an inert combined diff (routine sync-merge) recorded in new `GateResult.skipped_merge_inert` (no WARNING) rather than activating; squash-merge landing commits (parent-count 1) are never exempted. **M-1 [MEDIUM, ACCEPTED]** — `main.rs`'s `_ => {}` wildcard defeated `GateOutcome`'s deliberate non-`#[non_exhaustive]` exhaustiveness guarantee; routed to implementer for explicit per-variant arms. **M-3 [MEDIUM, ACCEPTED]** — a duplicate attestation heading (count ≥ 2) FAILed under the same label as a genuinely-absent one; new `FailReason::AttestationAmbiguous { count }` (Ruling 8(b) amendment) disambiguates, `count == 0`/`count != 1` boundary unchanged. All three (H-1/M-1/M-3) plus CR-2: fix-now, full implementer/test-writer specs in-ADR, no deferral. No gate predicate's PASS/FAIL boundary semantics changed outside the merge-commit combined-diff and duplicate-heading disambiguation described above. ADR-040 v1.18. Crate merged to `develop` via PR #777 squash-merge `19cb57e6` 2026-08-16 — **CRATE/IMPLEMENTATION HALF of [D-969]/[F-S2107-P10-001] now CLOSED; CI-WIRING HALF (Ruling 9(c) item 5 checkout-ref requirement) remains OPEN, tracked as a separate concurrent PR.** AMENDED 2026-08-15 (v1.19 — architect; PR #778 false-FAIL on post-merge push — defense-in-depth fix, both prongs human-ratified): new Ruling 9(f) — `GateOutcome::SkippedEmptyRange` (exit 0) introduced; `UnreachableCause::EmptyRange` RETIRED; empty/unresolvable range → `SkippedEmptyRange` exit 0 (was: `EmptyOrUnreachable(EmptyRange)` exit 2). CI job MUST include `if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'` (Prong 2 primary defense). §Decision 7 Ruling 7(a) trigger "(or `push:`)" removed. Ruling 8(c) five-outcome table updated. Ruling 9(a)/9(c)/9(f) updated. §Decision 10 reconciled. ADR-040 v1.19. | SS-05 | decisions/ADR-040-policy-15-attestation-gate-parent-sha-predicate.md |

| ADR-035 | Cross-site correspondence validation — three-tier architecture, fuel error taxonomy, and wasmtime version target: Decision 1 — three-tier architecture (Tier 1 WASM PostToolUse file-local — Class E frontmatter version↔last_amended↔modified[] monotonicity; Tier 2A WASM PostToolUse cross-site — Classes A+B BC-INDEX version-cite currency + STORY-INDEX three-way input-hash equality; implements BC-5.39.010; Tier 2B future `generate-index-rows` Rust binary operator-triggered; Tier 3 bats spec-validation reads factory-artifacts CI mount — Class C count/enumeration parity via parameterized Rust test; Class D-semantic finding-ID existence via adv-cycle-pass-*.md scan); Decision 2 — authoritative source declarations for derivable cells (BC frontmatter `version:` → BC-INDEX version column; BC H1 → BC-INDEX title column; story frontmatter `version:`/`input-hash:` → STORY-INDEX catalog cells; BC-version-pin is authored-record not derivable); Decision 3 — Tier 2B: `generate-index-rows` Rust binary operator-triggered, POLICY 21 compliant, not a PostToolUse hook (validators are read-only per BC-5.39.010 Invariant 1); Decision 4 — Tier 3 bats spec-validation suite reads factory-artifacts CI mount; full-corpus scan; `FORCE_FULL=1` escape hatch required; Class C via parameterized Rust workspace test; Class D-semantic via adv-cycle-pass-*.md scan; Decision 5 — fuel error taxonomy: `TimeoutCause::Fuel` is resource-policy error not validation result; dispatcher MUST emit advisory on fuel exhaustion (`[fuel-exhausted] plugin <name> ran out of fuel after <N> instructions — validation skipped`); `fuel_cap` optional per-plugin registry field required; `on_error = "block"` audit for `validate-factory-path-root` + `validate-input-hash`; Decision 6 — wasmtime version target: bump `"44.0"` → `"47"` (current stable; resolves RUSTSEC-2026-0149 out-of-support exposure); migrate to `"48"` LTS when released. Normative twin: BC-5.39.010. Research: `fd_readdir` available WASI preview1; missing `list_dir` is unimplemented host function. **PROPOSED 2026-07-30; ADR-035 v1.0. | ADR-034 | CI gate product-branch operand isolation and runtime-derived counts: Decision 1 — T-016 (`test_coupling_gate_story_gate_count_matches_bats_count_word`) MUST NOT read from `$fa_wt` (mounted factory-artifacts worktree); cross-branch `story_count` operand removed entirely; Decision 2 — `bats_count` replaced with runtime-derived count of inline `echo "DOC-PARITY FAIL"` lines within the AC-001(a) Write Discipline block (bounded by stable `# --- DOC-PARITY §Spec-Path Discipline: AC-001(a)` and `# --- DOC-PARITY §Spec-Path Discipline: EC-006 WARNING` section markers); **Do NOT count `_assert_doc_marker` calls** (those appear in §G.1 + Primary-paths layers only; count = 21, not valid for this gate); fail-loud when count is zero; verified count = 24; Decision 3 — `story_count` replaced with product-branch sentinel `# T001_GATE_COUNT=24` on its own line immediately after the `# --- DOC-PARITY §Spec-Path Discipline: AC-001(a)` opening marker and before the first gate code; T-016 fails loud when sentinel absent; declared value = 24; Decision 4 — T-016 remains required fail-closed check; fail-open options (b) skip-with-warning and (c) advisory job rejected under BC-5.39.008 v1.6 (fail-open never valid for governance gate that cannot read its target); Decision 5 — governance pattern for future coupling gates: spec-validation suites whose subject matter is spec content on factory-artifacts may read cross-branch (permitted by design); required coupling checks that derive equality-assertion operands MUST use product-branch sources or runtime-derived counts only; advisory coupling checks in non-blocking CI jobs are permitted with explicit advisory label. Adjudicates F-S2104-P29-H05 (cross-branch operand in required CI check) + F-S2104-P29-H01 (string-vs-string comparison, neither operand runtime-derived) jointly as a single T-016 rewrite.
